### 🎓 [Lunch Law Talks: Bail Laws](https://www.scu.edu.au/business-law-and-arts/events/lunch-law-talks-september/)
📆 Tue 8 Sep, 1:00 PM  
📍 Southern Cross University  
💰 See link  
`law` `justice` `debate`

Bail is a fundamental part of Australia's criminal justice system, based on the principle that a person is presumed innocent until proven guilty. Under the Bail Act 2013 (NSW) , courts must determine whether an accused person poses an unacceptable risk of failing to appear in court, committing further offences, endangering others, or interfering with the administration of justice. Bail decisions therefore require a careful balance between protecting the rights of the accused and ensuring community safety. Recent high-profile domestic violence and youth crime cases have intensified public debate about whether that balance is being achieved.

#### [Make-Believe Magic: The Worlds of The Jim Henson Company](https://www.qagoma.qld.gov.au/exhibition/make-believe-magic-the-worlds-of-the-jim-henson-company)
📆 Sat 12 Sep 2026, 10:00 AM – Sun 18 Apr 2027
📍 GOMA, Stanley Place, South Bank
💰 Buy Tickets
`puppetry` `film` `design` `exhibition`

Celebrating 70 years of creativity and innovation, the exclusive-to-Brisbane exhibition ‘Make, Believe, Magic: The Worlds of The Jim Henson Company’ will be an unprecedented exploration of world-building and imaginative storytelling. Founded by Jim and Jane Henson in 1955, The Jim Henson Company has created some of the most cherished characters in screen history and continues to revolutionise the art of puppetry for television and film. Their indelible imprint on entertainment, education and popular culture globally is seen in the much-loved series Fraggle Rock and Fraggle Rock: Back to the Rock, the treasured fantasy worlds of The Dark Crystal and Labyrinth, the iconic characters of the Muppets, and the instantly recognisable residents of Sesame Street. In a world-first, ‘Make, Believe, Magic: The Worlds of The Jim Henson Company’ will include the Great Hall set from the Emmy Award-winning television series Fraggle Rock: Back to the Rock, taking audiences behind the scenes of a working puppeteer’s film set. Immense and immersive, the exhibition will explore the Company’s dynamic trajectory from practical puppetry to today’s animatronic and digital innovations, as well as its ongoing dedication to the craft of creature design and trailblazing developments in on-screen puppeteering. It will feature hundreds of objects, from practical puppets and animatronic creatures to props, costumes, original drawings and moving image clips. Screening alongside the exhibition throughout its run will be three, free film programs at GOMA’s Australian Cinémathèque as well as a suite of QAGOMA’s renowned public, learning and access programs inviting audiences to explore and engage with the exhibition themes and artworks. This exhibition is organised by the Queensland Art Gallery | Gallery of Modern Art in collaboration with The Jim Henson Company. QAGOMA is excited to be celebrating the opening of ‘Make, Believe, Magic: The Worlds of The Jim Henson Company’ in association with Brisbane Festival in September. Works in this exhibition are protected under the Australian Government’s Protection of Cultural Objects on Loan Act 2013. Find out more.

This immersive and groundbreaking expedition takes you back to the golden age of ancient Egypt, allowing you to explore the depths of the iconic pyramids, meet the goddess Bastet, observe the embalming ceremony of Khufu, and much more! Embark on a journey to the past already experienced by more than 1 million people with the Horizon of Khufu! Highlights 📜 The largest immersive expedition of its kind dedicated to ancient Egypt 🧭 Travel back of 4500 years in time to the heart of ancient Egypt 🏆 Over 2.5+ million visitors worldwide 😎 Move freely through the Great Pyramid of Giza in a fully immersive virtual reality experience 🔎 Enter forbidden corridors, uncover the mysteries of the Queen’s Chamber, and meet the goddess Bastet 👨‍👩‍👧‍👧 Enjoy an intimate and educational experience designed especially for small groups 🌟 Developed in collaboration with Egyptologist Peter der Manuelian from Harvard University 🎁 Gift memories to someone special! Purchase a gift card here General Info 📅 Date: from March 2026 ⏳ Duration: approx. 1 hour, including 45 minutes of immersion walking 📍 Location: Fever Immersive Center - Level 1, 123 Gotha St, Fortitude Valley QLD 👤 Age requirement: 10+ with a minimum height of 1.30m (anyone aged 10-15 must be accompanied by an adult; anyone between 15-17 attending without an adult must bring a pre-signed waiver form) ♿ Accessibility: the venue is wheelchair accessible, but electric wheelchairs must switch to manual mode or alternatively use our manual wheelchairs onsite ❓ Please consult the FAQs of this experience here Description Put on your virtual reality headset and fasten your seatbelt! In Horizon of Khufu , travel thousands of years back to the heart of ancient Egypt. Discover the secrets of one of the seven wonders of the world through an innovative concept: the immersive expedition. Explore the Great Pyramid of Giza as it was more than 4500 years ago. Walk through its mysterious corridors, enter the Queen’s Chamber, and even try to meet the goddess Bastet, in a unique experience in Brisbane. Ready to discover how people lived during Egypt’s 4th Dynasty? Get your tickets now for Horizon of Khufu: Journey to Ancient Egypt in Brisbane!

#### [Convergent Bodies: First Leap](https://www.vacantassembly.com/events/convergent-bodies-first-leap)
📆 Sun 20 Sep, 3:00 PM
📍 Vacant Assembly
💰 See link
`art` `exhibition` `research` `ecology`

Image Credit: Bianca Tainsh (microscopy image with Sandra Tuszynska) Convergent Bodies is a visceral self-identification with other interspecies bodies and the emergence of a speculative posthuman future where technology is applied as ecological steward and interlocutor for entanglement with the more-than-human. In an 11-week OCCUPY residency, artist Bianca Tainsh has been visualising this concept through experimental juxtapositions of a vast exploratory archive of organic artefacts and digital documentation, and an accumulating body of aesthetic and conceptual research exploring microscopic and subterranean realms and the relational and metaphysical entanglement of all species. Unlike Haeckel's Art Forms in Nature , Tainsh's archive seeks the wild asymmetries of decay, sexual manifestation, and symbiosis. Convergent Bodies: First Leap is the initial manifestation of a new body of work that will take its cues from Tainsh's residency experiments. #block-f1e6d2ae56c80dd9e160 { } #block-f1e6d2ae56c80dd9e160 .sqs-html-content { } #block-f1e6d2ae56c80dd9e160 { mix-blend-mode: var(--tweak-text-block-blend ); #### [Snap Blak](https://www.qagoma.qld.gov.au/exhibition/snap-blak)
📆 Sat 30 Aug 2025, 10:00 AM – Sun 13 Sep 2026
📍 GOMA, Stanley Place, South Bank
💰 Free
`photography` `indigenous` `art` `sovereignty`

'Snap Blak' brings together dynamic examples of contemporary Aboriginal and Torres Strait Island photography that champion Indigenous self-representation, with assertions of visual sovereignty that resist prevalent stereotypes and re-present their people. Throughout ‘Snap Blak’ each photograph both subverts the settler-colonial legacy of photography of Indigenous people as an instrument of disempowerment and misrepresentation; while asserting identity, cultural continuity and belonging.

#### [The God of Small Things: Faith and Popular Culture](https://www.qagoma.qld.gov.au/exhibition/the-god-of-small-things-faith-and-popular-culture)
📆 Sat 20 Sep 2025, 10:00 AM – Mon 5 Oct 2026
📍 QAG, Stanley Place, South Bank
💰 Free
`faith` `culture` `art` `history`

'The God of Small Things: Faith and Popular Culture' draws its title from Arundhati Roy's evocative novel to explore the omnipresence of faith in the mundane and extraordinary alike. Centred around a rare collection of embellished oleographs by Raja Ravi Varma (India, 1848-1906), the exhibition delves into the intersection between devotional imagery and popular culture, capturing the divine as a living part of everyday life. Works from the QAGOMA contemporary Asian collection complement and extend this dialogue, through the juxtaposition of religious and vernacular iconographies with contemporary reflections on belief. QAGOMA has embarked on a landmark conservation and research exchange with colleagues in India to document and conserve a rare collection of Ravi Varma works. Specialists from QAGOMA’s conservation team will collaborate with the Museum of Art and Photography (MAP), Bengaluru, to share expertise and deepen cultural ties. QAGOMA’s conservation and research exchange is supported by the Centre for Australia-India Relations. QAGOMA is proud to be a recipient of a Centre for Australia-India Relations Maitri grant.

#### [Archie Moore: Kith and Kin](https://www.qagoma.qld.gov.au/exhibition/archie-moore-kith-and-kin)
📆 Sat 27 Sep 2025, 10:00 AM – Sun 18 Oct 2026
📍 GOMA, Stanley Place, South Bank
💰 Free
`art` `first nations` `history` `exhibition`

First Nations Peoples of Australia are among the oldest continuous living cultures on earth; Archie Moore’s kith and kin affirms this by tracing the artist’s Kamilaroi and Bigambul relations over 65,000+ years. The artist’s extensive drawing captures the common ancestors of all humans alongside animals, plants, waterways and landforms in order to emphasise our kinship responsibilities to each other and our surroundings. His choice of materials for this celestial map of names — fragile chalk on blackboard — addresses the need to disseminate First Nations histories and languages more widely. At the centre of the installation is a reflective pool, a memorial for First Nations individuals who have died in police custody, highlighting how Indigenous Australians are some of the most incarcerated people globally. The coronial inquests stand in as administrative markers of the departed, who are cradled by the watery reflection of the handwritten family to commemorate their ties to this vast web of relations. In a Kamilaroi understanding of time, past, present and future are co-present. By placing tens of thousands of years of kin on a single continuum, Archie Moore enfolds audiences within a First Nations understanding of time. kith and kin was awarded the Golden Lion for Best National Participation at La Biennale de Venezia 2024. The artwork was gifted to the Queensland Art Gallery | Gallery of Modern Art and Tate by Creative Australia on behalf of the Australian Government 2024. This presentation will be the first time the kith and kin has been exhibited since its debut in Venice, Italy.

#### [Sorcerers from Bohemia: Picasso’s Saltimbanques](https://www.qagoma.qld.gov.au/exhibition/sorcerers-from-bohemia-picassos-saltimbanques)
📆 Sat 14 Feb 2026, 10:00 AM – Sun 16 Jan 2028
📍 QAG, Stanley Place, South Bank
💰 Free
`picasso` `printmaking` `art` `history`

'Sorcerers from Bohemia' evokes the free-spirited world of the travelling circus performers, or saltimbanques — the subject of Pablo Picasso’s first major body of work in printmaking. 'La Suite des Saltimbanques' is a series of 15 loosely related etchings and drypoints that mark a transitional moment in the young artist’s personal and artistic development. Created on the cusp of Picasso’s Blue (1901–04) and Rose (1904–06) periods, the works are intimately connected with his paintings and drawings of the same era, including the Gallery’s own La Belle Hollandaise of 1905. On moving to Paris in 1904, 22-year-old Picasso encountered a bohemian crowd of acrobats, jugglers and street performers, whom he sketched as they rehearsed and socialised together. His early depictions focus on the vulnerability and poverty of these itinerant figures, whereas later works pay homage to their strong social bonds and the freedom of their creative lives. Appearing individually and in groups, Picasso’s saltimbanques vary widely in age and fulfil a variety of roles, from clown and friend to mother and king. Ushering in a lifelong experimentation with printmaking, 'La Suite des Saltimbanques' offers extraordinary insights into Picasso’s formative artistic concerns and the social milieu he inhabited at the time. In these sensitive and sympathetic portraits, he conveys the heft of a body, the texture of hair, a playful pose or a tender gesture with remarkable economy and elegance of line, ennobling figures who ordinarily existed on the fringes of society.

#### [Towards a Collapsing World: German Expressionism](https://www.qagoma.qld.gov.au/exhibition/towards-a-collapsing-world-german-expressionism)
📆 Sat 14 Feb 2026, 10:00 AM – Sun 16 Jan 2028
📍 QAG, Stanley Place, South Bank
💰 Free
`expressionism` `art` `history` `printmaking`

The early decades of the twentieth century witnessed an extraordinary moment in German art, as artists responded to a modern world in flux with works that engaged deeply with contemporary society, politics and culture. ‘Towards a Collapsing World’ explores this period of significant cultural flourishing through a survey of works on paper by leading figures of German Expressionism. Rather than a single, unified approach, German Expressionism was characterised by a plurality of attitudes and styles, with artist groups such as Die Brücke (The Bridge) and Der Blaue Reiter (The Blue Rider) forming and dissolving in response to fluctuating ideologies. Common to these disparate voices was a shared belief in the power of art to effect social change. The woodcut print became an important vehicle within German Expressionism, reviving a rich medieval heritage. Its stark, simplified forms — expressed through bold, flat patterns and raw aesthetic intensity —conveyed the inner turmoil and social critique of these expressionist artists with impassioned urgency.

#### [Screened to be Believed: The Jim Henson Company](https://www.qagoma.qld.gov.au/cinema/program/screened-to-be-believed-the-jim-henson-company)
📆 Sat 12 Sep 2026, 12:01 AM – Sun 18 Apr 2027
📍 GOMA, Stanley Place, South Bank
💰 Free
`film` `puppetry` `cinema` `animation`

For more than 70 years, The Jim Henson Company has delivered compelling stories and engaging world building that has captivated audiences for generations. By combining traditional puppetry arts with animatronics and real-time digital animation, the Company continues to be an unparallelled leader in the fields of practical and special effects-based performance for stage and screen. Presented as part of the ‘Make, Believe, Magic: The Worlds of The Jim Henson Company’ exhibition (12 September 2026 – 18 April 2027), ‘Screened to be Believed: The Jim Henson Company’ showcases productions made by the Company that shine a light on the vast array of technical skills and artistry used to create unforgettable cinematic worlds. Building from strong foundations laid by the Company’s founders, The Jim Henson Company continues to foster a culture of innovation, experimentation and exploration to forge new pathways in performance-led family entertainment, making for some of the most memorable on-screen moments in cinematic history. [sponsorLogos block] QAGOMA acknowledges the generous assistance of the National Film and Sound Archive of Australia, Canberra and the NBCUniversal Archives & Collections, Los Angeles in providing materials for this program. Program curated by Victoria Wareham, Australian Cinémathèque with additional film note writing by Rebekah Brammer.

#### [Beyond Henson: Puppetry and World Cinema](https://www.qagoma.qld.gov.au/cinema/program/beyond-henson-puppetry-and-world-cinema)
📆 Sat 12 Sep 2026, 12:01 AM – Sun 18 Apr 2027
📍 GOMA, Stanley Place, South Bank
💰 Free
`film` `puppetry` `cinema` `history`

Inspired by the work of The Jim Henson Company and Jim Henson’s Creature Shop, the film program ‘Beyond Henson: Puppetry and World Cinema’ surveys the rich international history of storytelling through the artforms of cinema and puppetry. Presented in conjunction with the ‘Make, Believe, Magic: The Worlds of The Jim Henson Company’ exhibition (12 September 2026 – 18 April 2027), the film program delves into crafted narratives that harness traditions of puppetry and cinema to move beyond belief and into the realms of the imagination. To trace the anatomy of onscreen puppetry is to draw from millennia of performance and craft traditions that extend across the globe. From the marionette puppetry of France and the Bunraku theatre of Japan, to the Wayang Kulit puppetry of Indonesia and the British Punch and Judy show, the democratised nature of the puppet theatre has enabled an unbridled style of performance that can enact the truthful and the taboo. Taking direction from the television series Jim Henson Presents the World of Puppetry 1985, the selected titles include films that precede (and sit adjacent to) productions by The Jim Henson Company, positioning the Company’s work within the broader canon of on-screen puppetry. [sponsorLogos block] QAGOMA acknowledges the generous assistance of the National Film and Sound Archive of Australia, Canberra; the Academy Film Archive, Los Angeles; the British Film Institute, London; the NBCUniversal Archives & Collections, Los Angeles; the Library of Congress, Washington; the National Film Archive, Prague, the Australian Film Television and Radio School, Sydney and the Revival House, Perth in providing materials for this program. Program curated by Victoria Wareham, Australian Cinémathèque with additional film note writing by Rebekah Brammer.

#### [Where the Wild Things Are Made: Jim Henson’s Creature Shop](https://www.qagoma.qld.gov.au/cinema/program/where-the-wild-things-are-made-jim-hensons-creature-shop)
📆 Sat 12 Sep 2026, 12:01 AM – Sun 18 Apr 2027
📍 GOMA, Stanley Place, South Bank
💰 Free
`film` `creatures` `craft` `cinema`

Presented alongside the ‘Make, Believe, Magic: The Worlds of The Jim Henson Company’ special exhibition (12 September 2026 – 18 April 2027), ‘Where the Wild Things Are Made: Jim Henson’s Creature Shop’ celebrates the skill and craftsmanship of the artisans at Jim Henson’s Creature Shop. Highlighting the diverse portfolio of expressive characters and creatures made by the Company for external productions, the selected films showcase the work of Jim Henson’s Creature Shop and how it continues to shape the landscape of cinematic storytelling. An important facet of The Jim Henson Company, Jim Henson’s Creature Shop is internationally renowned for creating pioneering techniques that bring extraordinary characters and stories to life. Formally established following the release of Labyrinth in 1986, Jim Henson’s Creature Shop continues to be an enduring creative hub where performance and craftsmanship is paired with experimentation and ground-breaking technical innovation to transform the imaginary into reality. [sponsorLogos block] QAGOMA acknowledges the generous assistance of the National Film and Sound Archive of Australia, Canberra; the Norwegian Film Institute, Oslo and the Revival House, Perth in providing materials for this program. Program curated by Victoria Wareham, Australian Cinémathèque with additional film note writing by Rebekah Brammer.

#### [Great and Small](https://www.qagoma.qld.gov.au/exhibition/great-and-small)
📆 Sat 21 Jun 2025, 10:00 AM – Sun 23 May 2027
📍 QAG, Stanley Place, South Bank
💰 Free
`animals` `indigenous` `culture` `art`

Animals have played a central part in Aboriginal and Torres Strait Islander culture and spiritual beliefs for over 60,000 years. Native fauna form an integral part of relationships with Country, in community and are a foundation of economies, identity and sustainability. Over many millennia of co-existence animals have been central to survival as a food source, for First Nations people they are also absorbed into lore and culture as images of totemic power; and into the ancestral creation stories that link people, land and animals. ‘Great and small’ is a celebration of these connections.

#### [Inscribing a Life](https://www.qagoma.qld.gov.au/exhibition/inscribing-a-life)
📆 Sat 27 Sep 2025, 10:00 AM – Sun 18 Oct 2026
📍 GOMA, Stanley Place, South Bank
💰 Free
`art` `history` `memorial` `collection`

‘Inscribing a life’ brings artworks from across the QAGOMA Collection that register existence, histories, and time through the act of mark making. From the brute force recorded in paint on a canvas that expresses strength and vitality, to the time commitment captured in the repetition involved in making intricate forms, the desire to record and share expressions of life is championed in this exhibition. Complementing the adjacent display of ‘Archie Moore: kith and kin’ both presentations share the themes of memorials and cosmic time. Alongside artworks expressing the intensity and wonder of existence are those that celebrate and solemnly honour lives that have come to an end. These artworks speak to the importance of acknowledging loss and how through observing death the public memorial reaffirms the social fabric for the living. The importance of thinking beyond human lifespan can be seen in expressions of the cosmos, 'Inscribing a life' features artworks that grapple with the vast scale and the deep time of the universe.

#### [Geoffrey Burns: Ride the Chronovore](https://www.parkercontemporary.com.au/exhibitions/geoffrey-burns)
📆 Wed 16 Sep, 12:00 PM – Sat 3 Oct
📍 PARKER Contemporary, 38 Hope Street
💰 See link
`contemporary art` `painting` `exhibition` `gallery`

Revisiting locations at different times of year, Geoffrey Burns approaches making as both a method and a measure of time. Works emerge through repeated encounters with place, where observation accumulates and memory takes form through surface and seasonal change. Ride the Chronovore considers time as an active force: consuming, transforming and leaving its evidence behind. Through repetition, Burns traces how surfaces shift, forms appear and recede, and environments register cycles that often unfold beyond human attention. Each return gathers new information while revealing what has altered, disappeared or endured. The fleeting span of a human life meets the slower tempo of the natural world, inviting an attentive and materially grounded way of looking at the quiet force of time. Opening Night Friday September 18 6 - 8pm VIP Preview from 5.30pm Geoffrey Burns in Conversation Sunday September 20 3pm - 4pm + celebratory drinks with the artist until 5pm Artist in Residence Saturday September 26 12pm - 4pm (drop in any time) Morning Musings Workshop Retreat Sunday October 4 10am - 12pm

📆 Wed 20 Sep 2023, 10:00 AM – Tue 26 Jan 2027
📍 QAG, Stanley Place, South Bank
💰 Free
`art` `collection` `whimsical` `digital`

The Collection Online team have been busy photographing thousands of artworks as part of QAGOMA’s Digital Transformation Initiative. This makes the Collection available and accessible to people everywhere, in detail never seen before. With dedicated conservators, registrars, technologists and photographers, the project has provided the resources to delve deep into Collection Storage to uncover and care for artworks not displayed in decades. This exhibition represents a small sample of our teams’ favourite finds – the weird and whimsical, the cute and curious. We gratefully acknowledge the hundreds of donors who generously supported the QAGOMA Foundation’s ‘Unlock the Collection’ campaign to digitise the Collection and make artworks more accessible and discoverable online including this diverse range of artworks, from small figures to small frogs. These are some of the artworks we have recently prepared for photography, and just some of the works which made us smile.

#### [Free – International Observe the Moon Night | 19 Sep 2026](https://seqas.org/event-6687519)
📆 Sat 19 Sep, 5:00 PM
📍 Barrett Street Park, Bracken Ridge, Barrett Street Park, Bracken Ridge — enter via Jude Street
💰 Free
`astronomy` `science` `family` `free`

SEQAS is proud to host International Observe the Moon Night, NASA's annual worldwide celebration of Earth's Moon. Each year, observers around the globe come together on the same night to share direct observations of our nearest neighbour, and this year you can join them right here in Brisbane's north. Urban Observers is SEQAS's free monthly community telescope night at Barrett Street Park, Bracken Ridge. Our members will have their telescopes set up and pointed at the Moon, along with planets, star clusters and nebulae. Beginners and families are very welcome, and you don't need any equipment of your own. Just turn up, look through the eyepiece, and ask us anything. Register your attendance with us and we'll provide you with an official International Observe the Moon Night participation certificate. Free for All ages. Bring the kids! Got your own telescope? Bring it along and our experienced members will happily help you set it up, collimate it, or get more out of it. Weather check: This event is weather dependent. Check our Facebook page from 4pm on the day for a go/no-go call.

#### [Volunteering with Paten Park Native Nursery](https://thegapsustainability.org/event/volunteering-with-paten-park-native-nursery/2026-09-08/)
📆 Tue 8 Sep, 9:00 AM
📍 Paten Park Native Nursery, 57 Paten Road
💰 Free
`nursery` `volunteering` `nature`

Volunteering can be done any day and time during the nursery opening hours. Please email or phone the nursery directly to organise your first time. Paten Park Native Nursery is a not-for-profit community organisation whose objective is the propagation and promotion of the indigenous plants of SE Qld. This is achieved through providing educational and training opportunities for individuals and community groups. Although the nursery has a paid skeleton staff (Manager, Production Team, and office support), it could not operate without the contingent of volunteers who perform a variety of essential tasks. Volunteers are encouraged to participate in all aspects of plant production. Many go on to specialise in tasks that they are most comfortable in performing. They learn about the myriad of plant species and their place in their ecosystems through, for example, seed collection and processing techniques in seed germination and growing from cuttings potting on young plants weeding recycling pots preparing plant orders customer relationships. Opportunities abound for fund-raising ventures (e.g. bush food jam and cake making) running environmentally-based workshops for children and adults, writing articles and developing material for our Web and Facebook Pages. With the volunteers already here, you are sure to expand your social contacts and make new friends. If these activities appeal to you and you wish to participate in a diverse community bound by commonality of purpose, PPNN will appreciate your contribution of whatever time you can spare (a half-day to several days a week) in whatever capacity. Do not hesitate to contact us personally, by phone or email.

For more information, please refer to our T&Cs Description This cruise to lunch will let you spend the day with two popular Brisbane identities; River City Cruises and the Breakfast Creek Hotel. Aboard the boat, the sights will be accompanied by informative commentary about the region as well as a morning tea to satisfy your on board cravings. The morning cruise will last for 45 minutes until you reach your next point of call; the Breakfast Creek Hotel. The Breakfast Creek Hotel is a popular hotspot for locals and tourists alike and after a short walk from the landing dock, it is where you can enjoy a delicious meal. Plating up a range of dishes such as Rump steak, Panko Calamari and Caesar Salad, it will be hard to leave the Hotel with a rumbling stomach. From here you will once again board the MV Neptune to cruise back to the dock at South Bank within the luscious Parklands.

#### [PETER MORÉN (Peter Bjorn And John, SunYears, Robert Forster’s band etc) intimate solo show! plus THE STIRLING CANON](https://thecaveinn.net/events/peter-moren-peter-bjorn-and-john-sunyears-robert-forsters-band-etc-intimate-solo-show-plus-the-stirling-canon/)
📆 Thu 17 Sep, 5:00 AM
📍 The Cave Inn, 47 Balaclava St., Woolloongabba, QLD, 4102
💰 AUD 0
`indie` `folk` `acoustic` `live music`

PETER MORÉN (Peter Bjorn And John, SunYears, Robert Forsters band etc) intimate solosshow! Peter Morén is the lead singer and guitarist of Swedish indie-icons Peter Bjorn And John. He has previously toured Australia with them and played among other things Laneways Festival and Splendor in the Grass. He's now coming back down under to be guitarist/backing vocalist in Robert Forsters Swedish band (Peter produced his latest album "Strawberries" in Stockholm) and finds the time to do some intimate lowkey solo dates while here. Peter has recently primarily worked with his two lauded album as SunYears (2023s "Come Fetch My Soul!" and 2025s "The Song Forlorn"), sitting somewhere in between solowork/band. SunYears is driven by Peter as a new angle and fresh start in personal songwriting after a long life spent in music. With a variety of sonics ranging from wistful folk, Beatles-esque harmonies, psychedelic guitar jangle to shimmering balladry the melodic wisdom of SunYears on record has brought in guestvocals from Ron Sexmith, Fruit Bats, Madison Cunningham, Lisa Hanningan, Nicole Atkins and more. SunYears recently played at the heralded Kilkenny Roots Festival, Ireland. Peter Bjorn And John is currently touring behind the 20th anniversary of their classic album "Writers Block" with dates in USA/Canada, Mexico and Europe. Peter also recently released a collaborative single with Josh Rouse and played guitar on James Yorkstons three latest albums. Live with just a guitar Peter is free to skip between SunYears material, covers of favorites and maybe the occasional Peter Bjorn And John-classic. No setlist and no boundaries! Opportunity knocks for a unique and spontaneous evening! Don't miss out! Peter will be supported by The Stirling Canon, young and keen and tremendous. [button link="https://events.humanitix.com/peter-moren-and-the-stirling-canon"]Tickets[/button]

`improv` `comedy` `duo`

One improvised show. Two sassy Aunties. Infinite judgement. The Asian Aunties think they know exactly what’s best for you — and honestly, they’re shocked you’ve survived this long without their guidance. Aunty Rani from Sri Lanka (Himali Ratnayake) and Aunty Maria from the Philippines (Janette McBride) are shimmying in with their signature blend of brutal honesty, invaluable wisdom, and unstoppable Aunty energy that can both bless you and roast you in the same breath. Write down your problems and put them in the rice cooker, so the Aunties can serve you their most generous advice. They’ll then improvise scenes inspired by your juicy dilemmas. The Asian Aunties are an improv comedy duo that was part of the sold out BrisAsia Comedy Gala at the Brisbane Powerhouse, they had a sold out show in Melbourne and a full house in Sydney, and they are the reigning champs in Big Fork Theatre's Cage Match with their 12-week winning streak. Audience members may be judged by the Aunties, but will not be brought on stage (unless you really want to), so you can enjoy the chaos from the comfort of your seats.

#### [Diagnonsense (improv comedy)](https://www.bigforktheatre.com/shows-events/zlc325x7ttpk7rz-9wrxf-r76dd)
📆 Sat 12 Sep, 5:00 PM
📍 Big Fork Theatre, 252 Saint Pauls Terrace
💰 See link
`improv` `comedy` `inclusive`

Brisbane's best improv comedy! Come see Brisbane's funniest and most endearing disabled and chronically ill improvisers do funny make-em-ups on stage for your entertainment. We know better than most that laughter isn't actually the best medicine, but it sure can ease the load of moving through a world that wasn't built for you. Diagnonsense is scheduled at an early evening showtime to help you manage your spoons. Head home early if you're spent or hang around for more hilarious comedy with Big Fork Theatre's regular Saturday Night Comedy programming. Big Fork Theatre is a fully accessible venue. The corner entrance to the building is stair-free, there's an elevator that will bring you up to the second floor and the flexible seating in our theatre can be adapted to accommodate any needs. Strobe lighting will never be used in a Diagnonsense show. Personal accommodations (headphones, stimming, etc) are welcome, we just ask that you respect the performers' needs and limit talking/vocal expression while the cast is performing. Laughing is encouraged of course! Please reach out of you have any particular accessibility needs you'd like accommodated. We can't promise everything will be possible but we'll always try. Diagnonsense is brought to you by a Little Spoons, a group of Big Fork Theatre community members living with disability and chronic illness. Through these shows, we hope to grow the visible representation of disability and chronic illness on Brisbane performing arts and comedy stages. In the process we might just happen to break down some stigmas, challenge some internalised ableism and encourage more inclusive practices across the arts. But no pressure! TICKETS ARE ONLY $10 ONLINE | $15 ON THE DOOR

For more than 50 years, American singer, songwriter and pianist Billy Joel has made an indelible mark on the American songbook. With 33 top 40 hits, 5 Grammy Awards and over 160 million albums worldwide, he is one of the most successful artists of all time. This September at the Brisbane Convention & Exhibition Centre, QSO under the baton of Jessica Gethin will celebrate Billy Joel in Piano Man. Directed by Mark Sutcliffe and starring Phil Burton, Alinta Chidzey, Jess Hitchcock and Josh Piterman, Piano Man features new orchestral arrangements by Nicholas Buc covering his greatest hits including Just the Way You Are, She's Always a Woman, New York State of Mind, Only the Good Die Young, Vienna and of course Piano Man. Featuring: Mark Sutcliffe director Nicholas Buc arranger Phil Burton vocalist Alinta Chidzey vocalist Jess Hitchcock vocalist Josh Piterman vocalist *Please note: This concert does not feature Billy Joel in person. Piano Man is presented in association with Make My Mark.

#### [8 Ball Aitken & Gwyn Ashton – ‘SUGAR RUSH’ Launch Tour](https://events.humanitix.com/8-ball-aitken-and-gwyn-ashton-cave-inn)
📆 Fri 18 Sep, 5:00 AM
📍 The Cave Inn, 47 Balaclava St., Woolloongabba, QLD, 4102
💰 AUD 0
`blues` `rock` `guitar` `live music`

8 BALL AITKEN & GWYN ASHTON (UK) are teaming up for a run of gigs to celebrate the launch their new guitar driven, hip-swinging, blues and boogie single SUGAR RUSH featuring Aussie rock icon Ross Wilson (Daddy Cool / Mondo Rock). Catch them at Brisbane's popular live music spot The Cave Inn (Woolloongabba) on Thursday September 17. Doors 6pm, music 7pm. ABOUT GWYN ASHTON (UK) Gwyn Ashton, one of the hardest working and powerful one-man bands, is back touring a new solo album titled Mojosoul and a brand new single Get Outta Town with Guns N’ Roses guitarist Richard Fortus and members of Australian bands Cold Chisel and Superjesus. In 2023 Ashton smashed stages across The Netherlands and Belgium with prog rockers Wishbone Ash, co-headlined the Adelaide Guitar Festival in Australia and relentlessly blazed his way across Wales, England, Serbia, Bosnia, Germany, Czechia, Slovakia and Poland. 2024 dates include an Australian tour and he’s booked for a mainstage performance at the prestigious Great British R&B Festival. Ashton is on the top of the list of the new wave of modern blues rock artists, as spearheaded by The Black Keys, Jack White, Seasick Steve et al. A first class entertainer he oozes stagecraft and showmanship, combining songs and stories from a road well-travelled with dextrous guitar playing, blasting out drum beats on Bill Ward’s old Black Sabbath drum kit, grinding fuzz bass lines with his thumb while he viciously attacks his guitar with a bottleneck slide, singing and blowing blues harp into a lofi radio microphone. Inspired by the likes of Hendrix and David Lindley, his “guit-arsenal” includes a rack full of electric and acoustic instruments including lap slide, resonator, 12-string and old pawn shop guitars. Ashton has had multiple Tracks of The Week in the UK’s prestigious Classic Rock Magazine from 10 of his commercially released albums on which he’s collaborated with Don Airey (Deep Purple), Neil Murray (Whitesnake), Robbie Blunt (Robert Plant) and Rory Gallagher’s band. “The most versatile performer we’ve had” - Deep Ellum Blues Club, Dallas, Texas. “The king of feel” - Robert Plant. “A true bluesman” - Johnny Winter. “A real player” - David Crosby. With these accolades and every recording garnishing numerous Albums of The Week/Month/Year from UK and European guitar and rock press Ashton’s incredible live show keeps growing stronger and he’s bringing it to a town near you soon! Formerly with Virgin Records, France, Gwyn Ashton was lead guitarist in Europe with Band of Friends (Rory Gallagher’s band) and in Australia with Stevie Wright (Easybeats) and Jim Keays (Master’s Apprentices). He has shared stages with BB King, Buddy Guy, Ray Charles, Peter Green, Van Morrison, Hubert Sumlin (Howlin’ Wolf), Mick Taylor, Status Quo, The Yardbirds, Slade, Robin Trower, Steve Hackett, Wishbone Ash, The Troggs and more. He has been invited onstage to jam with Mick Fleetwood, Canned Heat, Walter Trout, Elliot Randall (Steely Dan) and many others. ABOUT 8 BALL AITKEN 8 Ball Aitken has toured twenty countries performing his blues, soul, swamp-rock and slide-guitar. He’s earned a loyal fanbase from his live shows and internet videos, while paying his dues as a guitarist, singer, songwriter and producer. 8 Ball Aitken represented the Blues Association of South East Queensland at the 2026 International Blues Challenge in Memphis, Tennessee, USA with his band ‘8 Ball Aitken & Taya Chani’. The event was held over several nights on Beale Street and attracted 130 acts from around the world. 8 Ball Aitken & Taya Chani made the semifinals. 8 Ball Aitken won second place in the 2021 International New Orleans Cigar Box Guitar Festival. He has also taken home two Queensland Song Awards for blues and country album of the year from his album ‘Rebel With A Cause’. 8 Ball has lived on the Sunshine Coast for the last five years where he runs a recording studio in the Glass House Mountains. 8 Ball regularly tours Australia and USA. He moved to Nashville, Tennessee, USA between the years of 2012 to 2020 and worked with many of the best in the blues music business today. 8 Ball is back with a brand-new collaboration album ‘Nightbird’. He wrote and produced the recording with soul singer Taya Chani. The title-track ‘Nightbird’ immediately hit #1 on the Australian AMRAP Regional Charts in March 2025 for two weeks in a row as did their second single ‘Miss You The Most’ in May. 8 Ball & Taya’s signature harmonies and groovy guitars have proved to be a winning combination making them festival favorites. 8 Ball’s previous two self-produced albums ‘Ice Cream Man’ & ‘Ice Cream Man 2’ both hit #1 on the Australian Blues & Roots Charts. Tour dates, tickets, videos, merchandise and more are all available from 8ballaitken.com [button link="https://events.humanitix.com/8-ball-aitken-and-gwyn-ashton-cave-inn"]Tickets[/button]

#### [DEAD HEAD REDEMPTION – Album Launch](https://thecaveinn.net/events/dead-head-redemption-album-launch/)
📆 Sun 20 Sep, 4:00 AM
📍 47 Balaclava St, Woolloongabba, QLD, Australia, Queensland
💰 AUD 0
`album launch` `country` `rock` `live music`

It's been a hard road but this wagon train has finally pulled up! DEAD HEAD REDEMPTION are proud to launch their debut album THE WENDIGO HUNTERS here at THE CAVE INN on Saturday September 19th 2026. With support from fellow 4017-ers IRESON ROAD and THE COASTAL COWGIRLS, this will be a night of Hickory-smoked entertainment that's sure to tickle the nostalgia buds. NZ brother and tireless publican Adam Christy, of Cardigan Bar and State of Appalachia fame, brings his new project IRESON ROAD to their debut at the Cave. From gritty blues to powerful dark country ballads, we are set for an emotional ride into the sunset, accompanied by wailing guitar and heartbreaking vocals. Playful, talented, and gosh-darn rootin'-tootin', Sandgate's THE COASTAL COWGIRLS are a female-led bayside band that blend the dusty trails of country & Americana with the refreshing breeze of the coast. Delightful originals, river-washed classics, and yee-haw surprises are these boot-stomping blonde's buttercake. Closing the evening, DEAD HEAD REDEMPTION perform their brand-new album The Wendigo Hunters in full from start to finish. Evolved over the past three years, the songs evoke the atmosphere of the mangroves, the dankness of the swamp, and the harshness of the open land. Weaving tales of folklore, injustice, and murder, the songs tramp the trail between blues, outlaw, and pure rock n' roll. Following the closing track, they'll bring the night to a head with more originals, and a few rusty rail reworks for good measure! Our Concerts at QYO Series showcases Queensland Youth Orchestras’ ensembles and guest groups from across Brisbane and regional Queensland in the heritage-listed Old Museum Concert Hall. Presented three times a year, by each QYO ensemble, these concerts mark the culmination of each rehearsal term, giving audiences a chance to hear the progress and passion of Queensland’s young musicians. Each performance offers something new, from vibrant orchestral favourites to intimate chamber works, highlighting the diversity and depth of QYO’s musical community. #### [Vipoo Srivilasa: Express Yourself](https://www.qagoma.qld.gov.au/exhibition/vipoo-srivilasa-express-yourself)
📆 Sat 11 Oct 2025, 10:00 AM – Sun 13 Sep 2026
📍 GOMA, Stanley Place, South Bank
💰 Free
`kids` `art` `emotions` `interactive`

How do you feel on the first day of school? Or when you win a really hard game? What about when someone you love is upset? Artist Vipoo Srivilasa invites children to explore their emotions through drawing, dancing, and digital activities as part of his Children’s Art Centre exhibition. The project features a reprisal of Vipoo’s 2021 activity ‘Garden of Love’ along with fun new interactives, encouraging visitors to consider the spectrum of human emotion with curiosity and an open mind. About the artist Vipoo Srivilasa is a Melbourne-based, Thai-born artist who primarily makes ceramics. He is also interested in installation and audience participation. Positivity, beauty and accessibility are important attributes of his art practice, and he is particularly known for creating ceramic characters covered in flowers, called ‘flower bears’. General Info 📅 Date: Select your date directly in the ticket selector 🕒 Time: hourly from 10 a.m. – 10 p.m. ⏳ Duration: 40-50 minutes 📍 Location: Virtual Room Brisbane (Edward St, CBD) 👤 Age requirement: - Time Travel Chapter 1: 8+ (When played with an adult) - Time Travel Chapter 2: 10+ - Press Start: 12+ - Are We Dead?: 14+ - Asterix: Mission Potions!: 8+ (When played with an adult) 📱 For last-minute bookings (within 2 hours of your visit), please call +61 4 2250 0206 👥 Please note: 2-4 players required per game. As the last surviving Zombies, it’s up to you to stop the scientists from wiping out your kind. 💰 See link
`kids` `art` `painting` `creative`

West End Art Club starts in term 2 Can’t wait for school holiday art camp!? You can now join VA resident artist Chloë Waddell every Tuesday after school for some weekly art making. Tuesday afternoon classes will run on a 10 week term schedule with students being introduced to a variety of artmaking materials, skills & techniques across the 10 week class.Classes will be for a maximum of 8 kids to allow for plenty of one on one assistance. A large focus of West End Art Club will be encouraging participants to build their own creative identity and supporting them to realise their own creative ideas while introducing them to new materials, teaching new skills and offering new ways to look at things. Classes at the start of term will be more structured with specific goals and outcomes in mind. As the term progresses and participants build their creative confidence and develop preferences for materials and styles of making, they will have more freedom to explore their own ideas and spend more time using their medium/media of choice. The content of these classes will be flexible to allow us to meet the needs and interests of the children involved. It will include such activities as: drawing, painting, collage, printmaking, sculpture, assemblage etc. If your kids have attended one of Chloë’s workshops before, you can be assured they will be doing new things and exploring new ideas. All Chloë’s workshops are designed with the belief that art is a powerful medium through which children can explore, discover, and express their inner world in a supportive and fun environment. Overall, these workshops lay a solid foundation for your child's creative life and contribute to their holistic development. The emphasis on process and fun ensures that the joy of creation remains at the core of their artistic experiences. *SIBLING DISCOUNT - 5% DISCOUNT FOR BOOKINGS OF 2 OR MORE KIDS FROM THE SAME FAMILY **AFTER SCHOOL COLLECTION FROM WEST END STATE SCHOOL AVAILABLE If you have any questions that haven’t been answered here, please email Chloë directly at mschloewaddell@gmail.com #block-be38d6f9ccb3ebf80b74 { --stroke-style: none;--stroke-thickness: 6px; #### [ART TALKS – Beyond The Gallery: Art, Community and Connection with Katherine Kemp and Colin Bushell](https://rqas.com.au/event/art-talks-september-2026/)
📆 Sun 20 Sep, 2:00 PM
📍 Petrie Terrace Gallery, 3/162 Petrie Terrace
💰 See link
`art` `community` `talk`

[vc_row css=”.vc_custom_1523319263370{padding-top: 20px !important;padding-bottom: 30px !important;}”][vc_column][vc_column_text] For our next ‘ART TALKS’ event, the Royal Queensland Art Society Brisbane Branch invites its members and the art loving community to: “Beyond The Gallery: Art, Community and Connection” In this presentation and Q&A, Katherine Kemp and Colin Bushell, co-founders of Art & Eats, will examine how they have drawn upon local energy to break down community barriers and build a creative ecosystem, not just an exhibition. They share their successes, the pitfalls and future vision, providing advice on building a model others can follow. Art & Eats delivers the principle of showcasing creativity, whilst bringing together community beyond the gallery. They believe in partnership before ownership and purpose before profit. Katherine is the owner of Ruby Red Jewellery and co-founder of Art & Eats. She is also a Jewellery designer, small-business owner, entrepreneur, valuer, gemologist and MBA recipient. Colin runs Colin Bushell Photography and is co-founder of Art & Eats with Katherine. He is also a multi-award-winning photographer, writer and photojournalist; co-founder and former President of The Gap Creative; former Vice President, Professional Photographers’ Association of Queensland. This talk will be held at RQAS Brisbane Gallery on Sunday, 20th of September from 2pm to 4pm . Afternoon tea is included in the ticket price. Ticket Prices: – $5 for RQAS Brisbane Members – $15 for the Public Don’t miss your chance to attend this month’s art talks event and enjoy the fellowship of other like-minded artists – book your tickets via the link below now! Please note this event will be held in the main gallery space which is wheelchair accessible.
